I'm there, but I'm not really enjoying it. \n\nAnd it's the same at home. Even my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... doing those things just doesn't give me that little lift they used to. I'm just sort of going through the motions right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your ability to work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is. I do volunteer work, and... well, I've actually had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially, or just around the house, I've been turning down neighbors and friends a lot more than usual when they ask for help or want to get together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these symptoms are affecting your daily life most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say so. It's pretty much most of the day, every day. It's just this constant feeling that's always there in the background, from when I get up until I go to sleep.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the dance night. But... lately, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. I'm there, but I'm not really enjoying it. \n\nAnd it's the same at home. Even my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... doing those things just doesn't give me that little lift they used to. I'm just sort of going through the motions right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your ability to work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is. I do volunteer work, and... well, I've actually had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially, or just around the house, I've been turning down neighbors and friends a lot more than usual when they ask for help or want to get together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these symptoms are affecting your daily life most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say so. It's pretty much most of the day, every day. It's just this constant feeling that's always there in the background, from when I get up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure at all, even from things that would normally bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say I can't feel any pleasure at all. It just feels like it's always there.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks would you say you've been feeling this way, with symptoms like sadness and loss of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about a month. It... well, it started right after my sister died unexpectedly. So, yeah, about four weeks now that it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Well, not really. I mean, I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the dance night. But... lately, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. I'm there, but I'm not really enjoying it. \n\nAnd it's the same at home. Even my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... doing those things just doesn't give me that little lift they used to. I'm just sort of going through the motions right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your ability to work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is. I do volunteer work, and... well, I've actually had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially, or just around the house, I've been turning down neighbors and friends a lot more than usual when they ask for help or want to get together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these symptoms are affecting your daily life most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say so. It's pretty much most of the day, every day. It's just this constant feeling that's always there in the background, from when I get up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure at all, even from things that would normally bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say I can't feel any pleasure at all. I'm not really sure. I mean... like I was saying, it's more that things just feel very muted right now. I don't get that normal spark I used to, but I don't think it's completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of sadness are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I mentioned a moment ago, it really is every day. I don't think there's been a single day recently where I haven't felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness are significantly interfering with your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'd say it is. For instance, I've had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of a Juneteenth budgeting workshop I'm involved with. I just can't keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially... well, I've been turning down invitations from neighbors and friends a lot more than usual, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms are severe enough that you're unable to carry out even the smallest tasks without a lot of effort?\n\nPatient: Yes, it takes a lot of effort. Even simple errands or... or getting the papers together for the Cultural Center leave me feeling completely worn out. It's just like my body is moving through thick mud.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, thank you. I decided to make an appointment because... well, what really scared me enough to ask for help is that I keep having these mornings where I just think it might be easier if I didn't wake up. I want to be clear that I don't have any sort of plan to hurt myself or anything like that, but just having those thoughts on a regular basis made me realize I should probably talk to a doctor.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a month now. It started right after my sister passed away unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your interest in activities that you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the dance night we have. But lately... well, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. And even the things I do at home, like tending to my garden or cooking for the neighbors, they just don't give me that little lift they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have lost a few pounds. Food just doesn't really call to me much anymore. I'll fix myself a little toast or maybe some soup, and then... well, I'll realize by the evening that I've barely eaten anything all day. I'm certainly not trying to lose weight, I just don't have much of an appetite lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you've been moving lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, my energy has definitely been lower. It seems like even doing simple errands, or just... getting the papers together for the Cultural Center, it all leaves me feeling completely worn out. And physically, it just feels like my body is moving through thick mud lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't, since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything unusual. I'm just... very sad, and tired. But my mind isn't playing tricks on me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of sadness and not wanting to wake up are constant throughout the day, or do they vary?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't exactly noticed if it changes from morning to evening. It just... well, it feels like it's kind of just always there in the background. I haven't really paid attention to whether it varies at different times of the day.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness are worse at any particular time of day?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying, I really haven't noticed a specific time. It's just sort of... a constant heavy feeling. I don't think the mornings are any worse than the evenings, or vice versa. It just feels like it's always there.\n\nDoctor: How many weeks would you say you've been feeling this way, with symptoms like sadness and loss of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about a month. It... well, it started right after my sister died unexpectedly. So, yeah, about four weeks now that it's been like this.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Well, not really. I mean, I still make myself show up for things, like my book club or the dance night. But... lately, I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. I'm there, but I'm not really enjoying it. \n\nAnd it's the same at home. Even my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... doing those things just doesn't give me that little lift they used to. I'm just sort of going through the motions right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your ability to work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think it is. I do volunteer work, and... well, I've actually had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of the Juneteenth budgeting workshop for me. I just can't seem to keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially, or just around the house, I've been turning down neighbors and friends a lot more than usual when they ask for help or want to get together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these symptoms are affecting your daily life most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I would say so. It's pretty much most of the day, every day. It's just this constant feeling that's always there in the background, from when I get up until I go to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any kind of pleasure at all, even from things that would normally bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say I can't feel any pleasure at all. I'm not really sure. I mean... like I was saying, it's more that things just feel very muted right now. I don't get that normal spark I used to, but I don't think it's completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of sadness are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I mentioned a moment ago, it really is every day. I don't think there's been a single day recently where I haven't felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness are significantly interfering with your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'd say it is. For instance, I've had to ask another volunteer to cover parts of a Juneteenth budgeting workshop I'm involved with. I just can't keep up with the preparation the way I normally would. And socially... well, I've been turning down invitations from neighbors and friends a lot more than usual, too.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms are severe enough that you're unable to carry out even the smallest tasks without a lot of effort?\n\nPatient: Yes, it takes a lot of effort. This has been going on for about a month now. It started right after my sister died unexpectedly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that gave you even a small amount of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really. I just do them because I'm supposed to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
